# SECTION 10: CONTRADICTION RESOLUTION LOG
## Known Issues, Fixes, and Transparency

**Version:** TSE V2 (Phase 6 Validated)
**Audit Date:** 2026-03-17
**Validation Status:** PASS_WITH_WARNINGS — Production-eligible with documented resolutions
**Total Blocking Issues:** 5 (4 RESOLVED, 1 PARTIALLY RESOLVED)

---

## OVERVIEW

The Tribal Scoring Engine V2 underwent a comprehensive seven-phase rebuild process. Phase 5 rebuilt all 123 mechanisms against a Phase 4 scientific audit. Phase 6 validated that rebuild. The validation surface revealed five blocking issues — four mathematical or structural contradictions, and one design-level uncertainty — that had to be resolved before this document could be considered production-ready.

This section documents every contradiction that was found, the exact resolution applied, and the current production status. Nothing has been swept under the rug. Where a resolution is clean, it is marked RESOLVED. Where a resolution involves an acknowledged limitation, it is marked PARTIALLY RESOLVED with explicit scope. Readers who want to know exactly how much to trust each component of TSE V2 should read this section before using any score in a client context.

The governing principle here is: **a system with documented known limitations is more trustworthy than a system that claims no limitations.** The TSE V2 Phase 6 validation verdict was PASS_WITH_WARNINGS. This section explains every warning.

---

## PART A: ALL 5 BLOCKING ISSUES — FULL DOCUMENTATION

---

### ISSUE 1: TPC Ceiling Math Error
**Engine:** Tribal Proximity Calculator (TPC)
**Mechanism ID:** c1_84
**Severity at Discovery:** MAJOR (BLOCKING)
**Current Status: RESOLVED**

#### The Problem

The Tribal Proximity Calculator is a five-question rubric that generates a TPC multiplier applied to niche proximity scoring. Its structure is: 1.0 baseline + five Q-values summed on top. When TSE V1 was first constructed, the stated ceiling was 3.0, implying the five Q-values would sum to exactly 2.0.

Phase 4 audit identified that TSE V1's Q-values only summed to 1.55, making the actual maximum 2.55 — not 3.0. The ceiling was unreachable. Phase 5 corrected this by increasing Q-values: Q1 from 0.35 to 0.45, Q3 from 0.35 to 0.50.

However, the Phase 6 validation audit revealed that Phase 5's correction was incomplete. Adding the corrected values:

```
1.0 (baseline) + 0.45 (Q1) + 0.35 (Q2) + 0.50 (Q3) + 0.25 (Q4) + 0.25 (Q5) = 2.80
```

The document's own footnote acknowledged this 2.80 result, but the body text continued to reference "3.0 ceiling restored." This created a live contradiction: the math produced 2.80, the prose claimed 3.0. Any downstream formula anchored on TPC_max = 3.0 would systematically understate TPC contributions by 6.7% across all niche scores.

#### The Resolution

**TPC maximum is officially fixed at 2.80.** The reference to "3.0 ceiling restored" is incorrect and has been removed from the production document. All downstream normalization steps involving TPC are anchored to 2.80, not 3.0.

This required auditing every formula that used TPC as an input or normalizer. No other formula was found to use TPC_max as a literal constant — TPC outputs feed into the TP_adjusted calculation multiplicatively rather than as a percentage-of-maximum. The systematic error was therefore contained to cases where analysts were mentally estimating "how close to ceiling is this score?" — now corrected by accurate ceiling documentation.

The 2.80 ceiling does not compromise the utility of the TPC rubric. It means a niche with perfect proximity across all five dimensions achieves 2.80× rather than 3.0×. The scoring differential between a strong and weak niche remains fully intact. The rubric's discriminatory power is unchanged.

**What this means in practice:** A niche scoring 2.80 on TPC is at ceiling. A niche scoring 2.20 is at 78.6% of ceiling, not 73.3%. These are different numbers. Use 2.80.

---

### ISSUE 2: FAS Dual Rubric Incompatibility
**Engine:** Fear/FFWA Alignment Score (FAS)
**Mechanism IDs:** c3_33 (floor), c3_37 (ceiling)
**Severity at Discovery:** MINOR (but structurally blocking)
**Current Status: RESOLVED**

#### The Problem

The Fear/FFWA Alignment Score (FAS) was introduced in TSE V2 to capture how well a seller's content aligns with the FFWA (Fear/FFWA/Wait/Aspiration) state distribution of their target niche. A correctly FAS-calibrated seller speaks primarily in the language of the dominant FFWA state in their niche, rather than defaulting to Aspiration-state messaging that fails to resonate with Fear-state buyers.

During rebuild, two parallel scoring systems were developed for FAS and neither was retired:

- **System A (Categorical Bands):** FAS multiplier assigned based on number of active FFWA states matched. Ranges: 0.78 (no alignment) → 1.00 (partial alignment) → 1.15 (full alignment). Clean, discrete scoring with clear anchor points.
- **System B (Per-State Sub-Scoring):** +0.05 per active FFWA state that the seller demonstrates alignment with. Continuous accumulation model.

These two systems produce different scores for identical inputs. A seller aligned with three FFWA states scores 1.15 under System A (maximum band) but 0.15 above a 1.00 baseline under System B — producing a 1.15 score only by coincidence at that specific state count. For a seller aligned with two states, System A might produce 1.05 while System B produces 1.10. The outputs are genuinely different, not mathematically equivalent.

Phase 6 explicitly flagged this as "two competing rubrics for the same mechanism" — a direct violation of scoring reliability. No scorer could use the document without choosing between them, and the choice was not specified. Identical niches would receive different FAS values depending on which system was applied.

#### The Resolution

**System B is retired. System A is the only active scoring system for FAS.**

System A (categorical bands) is retained because:
1. It aligns with how FFWA states are actually assessed — categorical identification of dominant state, not per-state point accumulation.
2. The ceiling (1.15) was science-bounded in Phase 4 and matches the corrected FAS ceiling in c3_37.
3. The floor (0.78) was science-bounded in c3_33 — replacing the previous 0.80 floor.
4. Categorical bands are more resistant to subjective manipulation than per-state accumulation.

System B does not appear anywhere in the production Bible as an active scoring method. It is referenced here only as a resolved historical issue. Scorers should use System A exclusively.

The practical impact is that FAS scoring is now fully deterministic given a seller's FFWA state alignment profile. There is no ambiguity about which rubric to apply.

---

### ISSUE 3: ORTD Has No Scientific Basis
**Engine:** Object-Rule Trigger Density (ORTD)
**Mechanism IDs:** c1_76, c1_77
**Severity at Discovery:** MAJOR (BLOCKING for production use)
**Current Status: RESOLVED**

#### The Problem

Object-Rule Trigger Density (ORTD) measures the density of objects and rules within a tribe's material culture that trigger visceral identity activation. The original TSE V2 design assigned ORTD a 0.15 weight in a modified IRS formula variant that appeared in the main Section 4 formula stack.

Phase 5 audit classified ORTD with NO_SCIENCE_EXISTS status — meaning that despite the conceptual validity of the construct (objects and rules do function as tribal identity triggers), there is no published empirical literature that allows any specific numeric scoring of this dynamic. ORTD is a plausible theoretical mechanism that has not been measured, calibrated, or tested in any peer-reviewed context accessible to this audit.

The Phase 6 validation finding was that ORTD's modified-IRS formula was appearing in the production section despite [UNCALIBRATED] designation. This created a meaningful production risk: a scorer working through the main formula stack would encounter a formula variant that included ORTD and could apply it to a live client niche, producing scores contaminated by an uncalibrated variable. The formula looked production-ready. It was not.

#### The Resolution

**ORTD is moved to EXPLORATORY ONLY status. It does not appear in any production formula.**

The corrected document structure:
- Section 4 (main formula stack): ORTD does not appear. No formula variant includes ORTD as an active component.
- Section 8 (Exploratory and Future Mechanisms): ORTD appears with explicit [EXPLORATORY — NO SCIENTIFIC BASIS] tag. The theoretical basis is explained. The construct is preserved for future calibration.
- V3 Research Agenda: ORTD is listed as a primary candidate for empirical calibration if the scope of the V3 data collection project is extended to include material culture density scoring.

What ORTD conceptually captures is real. The tribal activation value of specific objects (Beck's cotton farm banner achieving 90% connection acceptance), rules (unspoken clothing codes at agricultural expos), and rituals has been observed in qualitative case data repeatedly. The problem is not that ORTD is wrong — it is that the specific numeric mapping (0.15 weight, scoring rubric increments) has no empirical grounding. Conceptual validity does not justify production use of unvalidated numbers.

Scorers who want to think qualitatively about object/rule density in a niche during a session may reference Section 8. They should not incorporate any ORTD score into a client's final niche IRS.

---

### ISSUE 4: IFS Normalization Denominator Uncertainty
**Engine:** Identity Fusion Score (IFS) — specifically the IRS_v2 normalization constant
**Mechanism:** IRS_v2_max compound calculation
**Severity at Discovery:** MAJOR (systemic calibration uncertainty)
**Current Status: PARTIALLY RESOLVED — flagged for V3 empirical calibration**

#### The Problem

The Identity Resonance Score V2 (IRS_v2) formula is:

```
IRS_v2 = IFS × (SSS + TSSM + TDS) × FAS × BAD
IRS_v2_max = 100 × (10 + 1.0 + 4) × 1.15 × 1.10 = 1,897.5
```

The Phase 6 audit verified this arithmetic: `100 × 15 × 1.15 × 1.10 = 1,897.5` — PASS. However, the normalization denominator used in practice is approximately 1,980, computed from slightly different input assumptions in some formula variants (FAS=1.20 in some earlier versions, not yet fully corrected to 1.15).

The deeper problem is structural: the IRS_v2_max value is computed from **theoretical input maxima stacked together**. Each input maximum is itself an estimate or a bounded value with a confidence interval. IFS = 100 is the theoretical maximum, not an observed maximum. SSS = 10 is the scale ceiling, rarely achieved. TSSM = 1.0 requires an explicitly multi-tribe-stacked entry. TDS = 4 requires four active tribe dimension signals. FAS = 1.15 requires perfect FFWA alignment across all states. BAD = 1.10 requires maximum brand anchor accuracy.

When you multiply six individually uncertain estimates together as a normalization constant, the compound uncertainty is much larger than any individual uncertainty. A 5% error in each input, compounded multiplicatively across six inputs, produces a normalization constant that could be off by 25-30% from the "true" maximum achievable score in a real niche.

This means that the relative scoring — "this niche is at the 75th percentile of IRS_v2" — is only as accurate as the normalization denominator, and the denominator is a theoretical construct, not an empirically observed ceiling.

#### The Resolution

**Acknowledged as estimation uncertainty. Formula used as-is in V2 with explicit documentation.**

For V2, the formula produces consistent relative rankings. Two niches scored against the same denominator will be correctly ranked against each other. The absolute scale (0–1,897 raw, normalized to 0–100 or 0–1000 depending on context) is a design choice calibrated against the theoretical maximum, not an empirically validated absolute scale.

This is a known limitation, not a bug. The limitation is:
- Relative comparisons between niches: VALID (denominator cancels out in ratio comparisons)
- Absolute score interpretation ("this niche scores 73 out of 100"): TREAT AS ESTIMATE
- Score-to-outcome mapping at upper ranges (15–22x): REQUIRES EMPIRICAL VALIDATION

**V3 resolution path:** Score 50 real niches through the full IRS_v2 process. Observe the empirical distribution of raw IRS_v2 values. Recalibrate the normalization denominator to the 99th percentile of observed scores rather than the theoretical maximum of stacked maxima. This grounds the absolute scale in observed data rather than theoretical construction.

Until V3 empirical calibration is complete, IFS normalization uncertainty is the primary known limitation of the IRS_v2 absolute scale. It does not affect relative niche comparison or ranking. It does affect statements like "this niche is worth 2.3× more than average."

---

### ISSUE 5: CSS vs. BAD Conceptual Overlap
**Engines:** Contamination Severity Scale (CSS) and Brand Anchor Density (BAD)
**Severity at Discovery:** STRUCTURAL (risk of double-counting)
**Current Status: RESOLVED — kept separate with clear definitional distinction**

#### The Problem

Both CSS and BAD operate as negative modifiers in the IRS_v2 stack. Both relate to signal corruption. At surface level, they appear to measure similar things:

- **CSS** measures contamination — the degree to which a niche category has been corrupted by bad actors, overpromising, failed products, or systemic category abuse.
- **BAD** measures Brand Anchor Density — the degree to which a seller's specific brand signal choices are correct or incorrect for their tribe.

The concern raised in Phase 5 was: are these two mechanisms measuring the same underlying construct? If a seller in a contaminated niche (CSS-3) uses the wrong brands (poor BAD score), are they being penalized twice for the same root cause — "their niche/category signals are corrupted"?

Double-counting the same negative signal would create systematic overpenalization in contaminated niches. A niche that is moderately contaminated AND has some wrong-brand signals would receive a compounded penalty larger than warranted. Niches near the contamination boundary would show artificially depressed scores.

#### The Resolution

**CSS and BAD are kept separate. They measure distinct constructs at different levels of analysis. No double-counting occurs.**

The key distinction:

**CSS operates at the category level.** CSS measures whether the *niche category itself* — independent of any specific seller — carries fear, skepticism, or negative prior associations in the minds of potential buyers. CSS-3 (Severe) means buyers have been burned so many times in this category that fear activates before any individual seller's content is processed. This is a market-level structural condition.

Example: "Online coaching for weight loss" is a CSS-3 niche because the category has been systematically abused by bad actors for decades. A buyer who has previously purchased three failed weight loss programs carries category-level Fear/Skepticism that fires on any weight loss coaching signal — regardless of the specific seller's brand choices.

**BAD operates at the seller execution level.** BAD measures whether *this specific seller* is using brand anchors with tribal precision. A tribal brand anchor is a cultural object, reference, or signifier that a true insider knows. Using the right brand anchor at Level 4 precision signals insider status. Using the wrong brand (especially a villain brand or tribal enemy brand) actively disqualifies the seller in the tribe's eyes.

Example: Beck (rural Australian agricultural consultant) using specific regional cotton farming equipment brands as casual references achieves high BAD — the brand choices signal she has inhabited that world. A consultant who references mainstream business brands in the same context scores low BAD — they're signaling outsider status regardless of their content quality.

These are not the same mechanism:
- CSS fires before any individual content is processed (category-level priming)
- BAD fires at the moment of specific content evaluation (execution-level assessment)
- A niche can be CSS-0 (clean category) with low BAD (wrong brand choices) — the seller fails on execution, not market structure
- A niche can be CSS-3 (contaminated category) with high BAD (perfect brand precision) — the seller's execution is excellent but they're fighting market-level fear

**Consolidation status:** CSS and BAD consolidation remains an open option for V3. One theoretical approach: model bad brand choices as a CSS-level trigger that elevates perceived contamination at the micro level. This is a design refinement, not a correction of a flaw. For V2, they are separate, distinct, and correctly applied.

---

## PART B: WHAT'S VALIDATED VS. WHAT'S AN ESTIMATE

TSE V2 is a composite system: some components are empirically validated, some are science-grounded but not empirically calibrated for this specific application, some are design choices made with theoretical justification. Users should know which is which.

### Battle-Tested Through Live Client Application

**The Identity Engine, Linguistic Engine, Cultural Engine, and RT = I × L × C × K formula** have been applied across hundreds of live client niche assessments. The directional outputs — which niches create recognition, which create confusion, which language registers trigger tribal resonance — have been validated through observed client outcomes over multiple years. These components are the foundation. Trust them.

**TSE V1 (25 mechanisms, 3-tier structure, 0–1000 scale)** was empirically validated against six real-world test cases. The validated finding: high-tribal niches outconvert low-tribal niches by a factor of 13.6×. This gap is not a design choice or a theoretical estimate. It was observed in structured comparison across real conversion data. The 13.6× figure is the empirical anchor for everything else in this system.

### Scientifically Grounded, Not Yet Empirically Calibrated

**TSE V2 new dimensions (TTSM, FAS, IFS, CSS, BAD, MM-10B)** are scientifically grounded. 72 of 123 mechanisms (58.5%) now carry science-backed values from a 12-discipline academic audit. The *direction* of each mechanism is supported by published research. The *specific numeric values* are bounded estimates, not empirically calibrated coefficients.

What this means: if IFS says a seller with deep tribal world encoding will convert better than a shallow one, that direction is validated by identity fusion research (Swann et al. 2012, Buhrmester et al. 2012). If IFS says the specific threshold between Level 3 and Level 4 is at score 76, that threshold is a design choice informed by scale theory, not a number derived from regression against observed conversion outcomes.

**CSS multipliers (×0.92, ×0.75, ×0.50, ×0.00)** have differential confidence levels:
- CSS-2 (×0.75): THE most scientifically defensible specific value in TSE V2. Directly derivable from Kahneman/Tversky loss aversion λ≈2.0 (95% CI [1.44, 2.31]). This is the only specific multiplier that has clean derivation from established meta-analytic findings.
- CSS-1 (×0.92): Consistent with Alter & Oppenheimer (2009) processing fluency meta-analysis (~60 studies). Well-grounded directionally.
- CSS-3 (×0.50): Direction supported by Baumeister et al. (2001) negativity dominance research (~100 studies). Specific midpoint value is a design choice.
- CSS-4 (×0.00): Logically grounded — terminal contamination means no scoring framework can overcome the block.

### Design Choices Made with Theoretical Justification

**TTSM exact increments (+0.12, +0.50, +0.75, +1.0)** — the concave stacking shape (diminishing marginal returns per additional tribe) is validated by cross-referencing Jablin (2001) on organizational assimilation across multiple groups. The specific values at each tier are design choices. A user who scores a triple-tribe niche at TSSM +0.75 is applying a well-structured design choice, not a calibrated coefficient.

**K-term gate thresholds (MM-14A ≥ 70, IFS ≥ 70, SSS ≥ 7)** — these three specific thresholds were set simultaneously with no individual derivation. The K-term mechanism itself (proximity trust boost when all three conditions are met) is supported by the Beck case (90% connection acceptance vs. 30–40% baseline). The specific numbers are the most structurally arbitrary thresholds in TSE V2. The boost value (K_base × 1.25) is a science-bounded estimate — down from ×1.30 in V1.

**Score-to-outcome upper range (15–22× at TP 850+)** — this range requires compounding five unverified mechanisms simultaneously. The 9–10× figures at lower tiers have empirical support from published DMA data. The 22× upper bound is a theoretical upper limit requiring: maximum IFS, maximum tribal stacking, perfect brand precision, clean category, and correct FFWA alignment simultaneously. Treat the 15–22× range as an upper-bound estimate pending V3 validation data.

**IRS_v2 absolute scale** — as documented in Issue 4, the normalization denominator is a theoretical construct. Relative comparisons are valid. Absolute percentile claims require V3 recalibration.

---

## PART C: V3 CALIBRATION ROADMAP

The following data collection activities are required to convert V2 design choices into V3 empirically calibrated values.

### Priority 1: IRS_v2 Normalization (IFS Denominator)

**What:** Score 50 real niches through the complete IRS_v2 formula stack. Record raw IRS_v2 values before normalization.

**Why:** Replace the current 1,897.5 theoretical maximum with an empirically observed 99th percentile. This grounds the absolute scale in observed data.

**Minimum data:** 50 niches, diverse across tribal strength and category contamination levels.

**Expected V3 outcome:** A recalibrated normalization denominator. The relative rankings are expected to be stable. Absolute score interpretations will become more reliable.

### Priority 2: TTSM Calibration

**What:** Identify 24+ real cases where tribe configuration is known (single-tribe, dual-tribe, triple-tribe) and conversion outcomes were tracked. Map TTSM increment values against observed outcome deltas.

**Why:** The current +0.12 / +0.50 / +0.75 / +1.0 increments are design choices. V3 should derive these from empirical outcome data.

**Minimum data:** 24 cases with known tribe stacking configuration and conversion or engagement outcomes.

**Expected V3 outcome:** Revised TTSM increments. Concave shape is expected to be confirmed. Specific values may shift.

### Priority 3: CSS Multiplier Calibration

**What:** Identify 10+ matched pairs of clean vs. contaminated niches (same tribe type, same seller quality, different CSS level). Track conversion outcomes per pair.

**Why:** CSS-1 and CSS-3 specific multipliers are design choices. CSS-2 (×0.75) is the only CSS value with direct meta-analytic derivation. The others need calibration pairs.

**Minimum data:** 10 matched pairs across CSS-1 through CSS-3 levels.

**Expected V3 outcome:** Tighter confidence intervals on CSS-1 (×0.92 ± ?) and CSS-3 (×0.50 ± ?). Possible adjustment of specific values.

### Priority 4: IFS Depth → Conversion Validation

**What:** Score 30+ sellers on IFS depth using the published rubric. Track conversion outcomes at 3 and 6 months.

**Why:** The IFS level thresholds (particularly the Level 3/4 boundary at 76, and the Level 4/5 boundary at 91) are logically derived from scale theory. The specific thresholds need conversion-outcome validation.

**Minimum data:** 30 sellers, spread across IFS bands, with tracked conversion outcomes.

**Expected V3 outcome:** Validated IFS boundary locations. If the current 76 threshold is correct, clusters of conversion outcome shifts should appear there. If not, the boundary will shift.

### Priority 5: ORTD Mechanism Development

**What:** Design a scoring rubric for Object-Rule Trigger Density using qualitative fieldwork methodology. Score 20+ niches. Assess whether ORTD scores predict tribal resonance outcomes independent of IFS and BAD.

**Why:** ORTD has conceptual validity but no empirical measurement framework. V3 can develop the measurement framework if the data collection scope allows.

**Note:** This is lower priority than 1–4 because ORTD's contribution to IRS_v2 is not currently included in any production formula. V3 calibration of ORTD would be additive to the formula, not corrective.

### V3 Timeline Structure

These five calibration studies can be run sequentially within a single scoring project if the project systematically tracks:
1. Niche tribal configuration (tribe type, stacking, IFS of seller)
2. Category contamination level (CSS assessment at intake)
3. Brand anchor quality (BAD assessment at intake)
4. Conversion outcomes at 90 days and 180 days
5. FFWA state alignment of content (FAS assessment at intake)

A structured niche scoring project with 50 clients minimum would provide sufficient data for Priority 1–4 calibration within a single cohort.

---

## PART D: TRANSPARENCY STATEMENT

### What You Should Trust Fully

1. **The directional findings of the engine** — high-tribal niches outperform low-tribal niches. This is validated empirically (13.6× conversion gap, 6 test cases). The direction is real.

2. **The Identity, Cultural, and Linguistic Engine outputs** — years of live client application have validated that these engines correctly identify whether a seller's positioning, language, and cultural signals will create tribal recognition or tribal friction. These are the foundations of the system.

3. **CSS-2 (×0.75)** — the most scientifically defensible specific value in TSE V2. Directly derivable from the most replicated finding in behavioral economics (Kahneman/Tversky loss aversion, λ≈2.0). If any number in this system is "correct," it is this one.

4. **The TPC ceiling of 2.80** — after correction from the math error documented in Issue 1. This number is simply what the arithmetic produces. It is not an estimate.

5. **Relative niche rankings** — if Niche A scores higher than Niche B on IRS_v2, that ranking is meaningful and reliable. The normalization denominator uncertainty affects absolute values, not relative comparisons.

### What You Should Treat as Science-Grounded Design Choices

1. **All TTSM increment values (+0.12, +0.50, +0.75, +1.0)** — directionally correct, specific values await V3 calibration.

2. **K-term gate thresholds (MM-14A ≥ 70, IFS ≥ 70, SSS ≥ 7)** — structurally designed, not empirically derived. The boost mechanism is real; the exact gates are the most arbitrary specific values in the system.

3. **IFS level boundaries (at 20, 35, 55, 75, 90)** — derived from scale theory and identity fusion depth research. Specific boundary locations are design choices pending V3 validation.

4. **CSS-1 (×0.92) and CSS-3 (×0.50)** — directionally supported by research, specific values are design choices. CSS-3 in particular has a meaningful confidence interval pending calibration data.

5. **Score-to-outcome multipliers at TP 700+ (10–22×)** — upper bound estimates. The 9–10× figures at TP 600+ have empirical support. The 15–22× figures at TP 850+ require five mechanisms to compound simultaneously. Treat as theoretical upper bounds.

### What You Should Flag Before Presenting to Clients

1. **Any absolute IRS_v2 percentile claim** — "your niche scores at the 78th percentile" is an estimate, not a calibrated finding. Relative comparisons ("your niche scores 2.3× higher than the industry average") are more defensible but still carry normalization uncertainty.

2. **Score projections using ORTD** — ORTD is exploratory. Any formula that includes ORTD has not been validated for production use. If you encounter an IRS formula variant with an ORTD term, do not present that to clients.

3. **Any claim that a specific niche will achieve 20× conversion** — this requires empirical validation that does not yet exist. Present conversion range estimates as what they are: projections from a validated engine, not guaranteed outcomes.

### The Bottom Line

TSE V2 is a high-quality, science-grounded niche scoring system. 72 of 123 mechanisms (58.5%) are now anchored in published academic research across 12 disciplines. All five blocking issues discovered in Phase 6 validation have been either resolved or explicitly documented with a clear V3 calibration path.

The engine will correctly identify which niches have strong tribal structure and which do not. It will correctly rank niches against each other. It will correctly flag contamination, misalignment, and shallow tribal encoding. The absolute numeric outputs should be treated as structured estimates that become more precise with each data collection cycle.

Use the system with confidence. Document the known limitations when presenting to clients. Use V3 calibration data when it becomes available to refine the estimates into empirically grounded values.

---

*End of Section 10 — Contradiction Resolution Log*
